Scott McTominay given police escort as drama 36 hours before Scotland World Cup clash laid bare

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Scott McTominay has revealed he had to have a police escort to a hospital in Boston to have his heart ‘shocked’ 36 hours before Scotland’s opening World Cup game against Haiti.

At the time, his absence from training and travel arrangements to Boston – he travelled from the team’s base in Charlotte separately from the rest of the team – were attributed to a stomach bug. In fact, it was a heart condition known as atrial fibrillation.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

“You're not going to say I have something to do with my heart two days before the first game of the World Cup,” he said.

“It's not an ideal scenario being in the hospital two days before—well, technically 36 hours before the first game of the World Cup. But you have to take the cards that you're dealt on the chin. It's part of the game. There's nothing I could do about it.”

Some will question the decision to let him play in not only the match against Haiti, which Scotland won 1-0, but also the two following group games against Morocco and Brazil. The Scots lost both fixtures and exited the World Cup with McTominay perceived to have been under par.

“It was in my hands,” he said. “The medical team reassured me it was safe to play and they said: ‘What do you think?' I said: ‘Let’s go for it’.”

Unbeknown to nearly everyone, and while Scotland fans were drinking bars dry in Boston, McTominay was at the centre of incredible drama.

Manager Steve Clarke told reporters that the midfielder had been laid low by a stomach bug. The reality of the situation - and the fact it was kept secret for so long - is barely believable given the global attention on the tournament and that McTominay is Scotland's star player.

He himself sought to downplay the situation on Tuesday at Hampden as he revealed the true story for the first time. “We got a police escort to go and get the shock then that was it to be honest,” recalled McTominay. “After that, I was back in normal rhythm, which is how a healthy heart should be then I went and played the game two days later.”

He described being put under anaesthetic. “You have to get put to sleep,” he said. “Whenever you go under anaesthetic and you have to get put to sleep, it decreases the athletic performance a little bit and (affects) sleep and stuff like that. It was far from ideal but those are the cards that you're dealt, there's nothing you can do about it.”

Although he stressed his family were fully on board with him playing, he wouldn’t have listened had it been otherwise. “All my family and friends had just flown 10 hours for the first game of the World Cup,” he said.

“I didn’t sit there thinking I might not play. That would never be the case. I was always going to play in that game. Maybe selfishly, I don’t know but that’s football. Players play with injuries all the time when they’re not quite right and it might be to the detriment of the team. (But) I was fully functional.”

The Napoli midfielder has since undergone an operation after the problem resurfaced following an early season Serie A game against Como. He returned to Manchester, where he had a procedure known as pulsed field ablation.

The player was sidelined for several weeks, missing the first games under new Scotland manager Sebastien Pocognoli but has returned to the squad for upcoming Nations League games against North Macedonia and Switzerland.

Galderisi vota Amorim: "Bisogna aspettare gli allenatori, oggi non si guarda più il lavoro giornaliero"

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ai microfoni di Tuttomercatoweb, Giuseppe Galderisi, ex giocatore tra le tante di Hellas Verona, Milan, Juventus e Lazio, ha rilasciato una lunga intervista in occasione del Premio La Clessidra ad Anghiari. Di seguito vari estratti delle sue parole anche sul Milan allenato da mister Ruben Amorim:

"Mi piace molto come allenatore e persona perchè diretto e schietto. Nel calcio bisogna avere pazienza ed essere bravi ad aspettare le persone in cui si crede. Amorim dipende da un pareggio, da un palo interno o da un palo esterno, non si guarda più il lavoro giornaliero".

Il Milan di Ruben Amorim corre più forte rispetto a quello guidato da Massimiliano Allegri nella scorsa stagione. Dopo 11 giornate i rossoneri hanno raccolto 24 punti, contro i 22 conquistati da Max un anno fa. Il successo sul Genoa di domenica ha portato il Diavolo al quarto posto, scavalcando il Como sconfitto a Milano contro l'Inter, tenendo a debita distanza Juventus, Napoli ed Atalanta ed accorciando sulla Lazio capolista. Amorim viaggia a 2,18 punti di media a partita, con una proiezione finale di 83 punti. Numeri incoraggianti, anche se il Milan deve trovare maggiore continuità e solidità a fine gara.

Grupo Cibest (CIB) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nequi's spinoff is complete and its loan book is expanding fast.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Grupo Cibest S.A. (CIB -3.96%) reported third quarter results that included loan growth acceleration and the completion of the Nequi spinoff. Management stated that commercial lending served as the primary driver of portfolio expansion as demand for new projects in Colombia increased. The company revised its full-year loan growth guidance upward to reflect this stronger demand. The transition of Nequi into an independent commercial financing company was completed on Sept. 1, and the entity now serves 29 million customers. Management indicated that capital allocation strategies, including the divestment of Banistmo and new capital instrument issuances, are intended to optimize the group's structure for long-term returns.

Operator: Good morning, ladies and gentlemen, and welcome to the Grupo Cibest Bancolombia Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Conference Call. My name is Carrie, and I will be your operator for today's call. [Operator Instructions] Please note that this conference is being recorded. Please note that this conference call will include forward-looking statements, including statements related to our future performance, capital position, credit-related expenses and credit losses. All forward-looking statements, whether made in this conference call and future filings in press releases or verbal address matters that involve risks and uncertainties.

Consequently, there are factors that could cause actual results to differ materially from those indicated in such statements, including exchanges in general economic and business conditions, changes in currency exchange rates and interest rates, introductions of competing products by other companies, lack of acceptance of new products or services by our targeted clients, changes in business strategy and various other factors that we describe in our reports filed with the SEC. With us today is Mr. Juan Carlos Mora, Chief Executive Officer; Mr. Mauricio Botero Wolff, Chief Strategy and Financial Officer; and Ms. Laura Clavijo, Chief Economist. I will now turn the call over to Mr. Juan Carlos Mora, Chief Executive Officer. Mr. Juan Carlos, you may begin.

Juan Uribe: Good morning, and thank you for joining Grupo Cibest's Third Quarter Conference Call. Please turn to Slide 2. In the third quarter, Colombia faced a difficult political, social and economic environment. A tense government transition was followed by a major earthquake in the Southwest. This increased fiscal concern and raise questions about how quickly and on what scale, reconstruction can restore infrastructure, economic activity and community well-being. Overall, we now expect the economy to have grown 2.1% in the third quarter, below earlier forecasts. Laura Clavijo will elaborate on this shortly. Even so, we delivered strong results.

Credit origination rebound sharply, led by commercial loans as confidence grew in the rule of law and security and as expectations rose for a credible path to fiscal adjustment. Net income reached COP 2 trillion in the quarter. Year-to-date, it is up 10.5%, more than offsetting Banistmo's contribution last year. NIM ended the quarter at 7.4%. The Investment income stabilized, time deposits resumed growth and lower spread commercial loans led the expansion on the loan book. Cost of risk rose 1.9% on higher consumer provisions. This was as expected and within our guided range. The strict expense discipline also underpinned solid operating performance. As a result, ROE for the quarter stood at 21.7%.

On September 1, we reached another corporate milestone. Separation of Nequi from Bancolombia. Nequi is Colombia's leading neo bank with a growing presence in Central America. It is now an independent commercial financing company, wholly owned by Grupo Cibest. This spinoff is designed to accelerate its development as a leading regional neo bank. In July, we also completed a intra-Group additional Tier 1 capital instrument transaction to further optimize Bancolombia's capital structure. I will now turn the call over to our Chief Economist, Laura Clavijo for an overview of the macroeconomic landscape. Laura?

Laura Clavijo: Thank you, Juan Carlos. If you could please proceed to Slide 4. Turning to Colombia, the economy enters 2027 with a mixed but gradually improving outlook. Our central view is that the year will be characterized by two competing forces, an improving investment cycle and post-earthquake reconstruction on the one hand and the necessary adjustment in fiscal and monetary conditions on the other. We expect GDP growth to moderate from 2.6% in 2026 to 2.4% in 2027. Household consumption should remain an important source of growth, but lose momentum as high interest rates, persistent inflation and softer labor market conditions constrained disposable income. At the same time, fiscal consolidation will reduce the contribution from government spending.

Encouragingly, the composition of growth should become more investment-oriented. This investment is projected to rise above 17% of GDP, supported by construction, mining and energy projects as well as reconstruction following the August earthquake. Inflation remains one of the key challenges, following a projected 6.7% year-end reading in 2026, headline inflation could peak at 7.3% during the first half of 2027, largely reflecting the impact of El Niño on food and regulated prices, together with indexation and the minimum wage adjustments. We expect these supply side pressures to fade during the second half, bringing inflation down to 5.5% by December. Nevertheless, still well above the Central Bank's target range. This inflation backdrop will keep monetary conditions tight.

We suspect the policy rate could rise further to 12.5% and remain at this level well into 2027 with the Central Bank waiting for clear evidence of disinflation before restarting the easing cycle. We, therefore, anticipate only modest cuts in the fourth quarter while fiscal risks and elevated global rates further constrained the scope for monetary easing. The fiscal position remains the main macroeconomic vulnerability. An ambitious economic rescue bill worth 2.4% of GDP, containing a significant budget cut and additional taxes are being discussed in Congress before year-end. The signals of fiscal responsibility but be weighed against significant expenditure rigidities and historically high financing requirements.

Our baseline scenario speaks of a Central Government deficit of 7.9% of GDP in 2027, compared with 7.2% in 2026 and assumes a fiscal adjustment of at least 1.5% of GDP to maintain investor confidence and set the path towards fiscal sustainability. Finally, external accounts remain manageable, although the current account deficit is expected to widen from 2.5% of GDP in 2026 to 3.2% in 2027. Importantly, remittances of around 3.4% of GDP should remain an important buffer, keeping the external deficit below its historical average of approximately 4%. Overall, 2027 should be viewed less of a year of acceleration and more of a year of transition.

Growth remains resilient, and its composition improves but Colombia will need to navigate sticky inflation, restrictive financial conditions and above all, a demanding fiscal adjustment before a more balanced and sustainable expansion can take hold. If you could please proceed to Slide 5. Turning to Central America. The outlook for El Salvador and Guatemala remains constructive, although inflationary pressures warrant attention. In El Salvador, growth accelerated to 5.1% year-over-year in the second quarter of 2026, increasingly supported by investment and double-digit construction growth. While resilient consumption, tourism revenues and renewed progress under the IMF program provide additional buffers despite lower remittances and higher import costs. In Guatemala, growth remained robust at 4.3%, supported by domestic demand, remittances, manufacturing and exports.

However, inflation has risen to 4.7%, while the medium-term outlook increasingly hinges on translating fiscal expansion and the strengthened public-private partnership framework into infrastructure investment. Alongside institutional improvements that could support the country's path toward investment grade. Now please let me turn the presentation to Mauricio, who will present Nequi as an independent entity followed by Grupo Cibest's quarterly performance.

Mauricio Botero Wolff: Thank you, Laura. Please proceed to Slide #7. Nequi was created 10 years ago with the purpose of improving the way people and business interact with money, and since its inception, its success as a powerful and scalable financial inclusion platform has been compelling with a customer base of more than 29 million users in an activity ratio above 80%. And Nequi was launched in 2016, offering a basic marketplace for top-ups and bill payments. Leveraging on the user's transactional data and cash flow dynamics. In 2019, Nequi launched its first credit product, a significant first step towards the financial evolution of individuals without prior credit history.

The pandemic marked a turning point in Nequi history, as it was one of the channels that the government selected to disburse subsidies, eventually driving fast, massive user engagement, expanding by 4.5x its user base in only 3 years. Since then, it has continued enhancing its marketplace and its value proposition has evolved from individuals towards small business cash management solutions and interoperability. Also, it initiated its regional expansion into El Salvador and Guatemala. Please turn to Slide 8. Its business model is built around three core services that foster engagement, generate high transaction volumes and capture a stable, low-cost operational deposits, complemented by a growing financial portfolio, a marketplace, B2B and cross-border solutions.

As of third quarter, 24 million customers are active users, out of which 18 million are monetized. Moreover, 7 out of 10 Colombian adults have a Nequi account and 3.9 million users of Nequi as their only account. 65% of Nequi account holders are not Bancolombia customers and 42% of payments in Bre-B ecosystem come from Nequi. In fact, exponential growth in user engagement and transaction volumes drove operating leverage and cost dilution, enabling Nequi to reach breakeven in late 2025. Please turn to Slide #9. From an accounting perspective, Nequi's stand-alone financials reflect its balance sheet as of September 30, while the income statement captures only 1 month of full operations as the spinoff was executed on September 1.

Thus, in the following slides, we provide a set of administrative financials and performance metrics for the third quarter and previous quarters on a stand-alone basis, intended only for comparative purposes. Deposits closed at COP 6.9 trillion, down 9.5% quarter-over-quarter as balances normalized of the midyear service bonus and up 24% year-over-year. On the other hand, its loan book expanded 12% in the quarter reaching COP 2.5 trillion. Following the spin-off, Grupo Cibest allocated COP 600 billion of capital to Nequi implying a total solvency ratio of 13%. All in all, Nequi initiates this new phase as an independent entity with a robust balance sheet to support scalable growth going forward. Please turn to Slide 10.

Net interest income increased 5.3% in the quarter due to loan expansion and a COP 20 million increase in interest income as a one-off related to the spin-off. Accordingly, NIM reached 18.1%. Absent of the one-off NIM would have been 16.8%. This represent a significant and diversified source of overall income and continued growing at a steady pace during the quarter. The net fee income ratio closed at 22.6%. From a credit loss perspective, provision expense amounted to COP 145 billion, an increase explained by higher loan origination in a one-off related to the transition between former credit model under local GAAP and the current IFRS accounting.

Thus, cost of risk reached 24.6%, which absent of the one-off, would have remained flat at 16.9% and in line with our expectations given the pace of loan growth. Please turn to Slide 11. ARPAC continues to grow while cost to serve continues to decline, such that ARPAC now represents 2.3x CTS demonstrating the strong operating leverage embedded in its business model. All in all, third quarter's net income was COP 33 billion, equivalent to a 23% ROE. Absent of one-offs related to the spin-off, net income would have reached COP 41 billion, which represents an ROE of 28%. Please turn to Slide 12.

Finally, we expect ROE to reach 28% in 2026 and between 28% and 30% in 2027, driven by loan growth that will support margin expansion while provision expenses evolve consistently with portfolio growth. Please turn to Slide 14. Now Grupo Cibest's gross loan portfolio reached COP 274 trillion, increasing 4.3% quarter-over-quarter and 9.7% and year-over-year, which absent of FX, would have been 13.5%, reflecting a strong underlying business dynamic. Commercial loans were the main growth driver, expanding 5.5% during the quarter as per a more constructive business environment in Colombia. Consumer loans also grew at a faster pace, increasing 2% in the quarter, mainly driven by unsecured personal loans and credit cards.

Meanwhile, mortgages maintaining their positive trend, expanding 2.1% over the quarter. Please proceed to Slide 15. When broken down by entity, Bancolombia continued to drive growth supported by commercial loans. The contraction in the consumer portfolio is explained by the spin-off of Nequi, while Nequi continued to show solid origination dynamics, growing 12% during the quarter. Bancoagricola maintained a positive trend, while BAM declined mainly in commercial loans, yet its consumer portfolio is showing signs of recovery with September posting the strongest month-over-month growth of the year. Cross-border lending continued to expand through our offshore operations, allowing us to provide competitive foreign currency financing across different geographies.

As of September, BAM's cross-border loan portfolio grew 63% year-over-year, while Bancoagricola expanded 13%. Please proceed to Slide 16. Total deposits reached COP 275 trillion, growing 1.5% quarter-over-quarter and 8.6% year-over-year. Excluding FX, annual growth would have been 13%. Bancolombia's growth came primarily from time deposits to offset part of the deposit migration resulting from Nequi's spinoff. The decline in savings accounts reflect seasonality related to payroll and employee benefit payments. At Bancoagricola and BAM, deposit growth was mainly driven by sight deposits. Please proceed to Slide 17.

Our funding mix remained resilient, supported by the strength of our sight deposit base, which represents 57% of total funding and provides a structural advantage in containing funding costs in the current interest rate environment. The cost of deposits increased slightly to 4.5%, reflecting Colombia's higher interest rate and the greater share of institutional savings accounts and time deposits. Please proceed to Slide 18. Net interest income reached COP 5.7 Trillion, decreasing 4.9% over the quarter, equivalent to a NIM of 7.4%. The drivers of the quarterly decrease are twofold. First, the normalization of treasury income following an exceptionally strong second quarter.

And second, lending NIM contraction due to higher funding needs related to Nequi's spin-off and to the positive dynamic of the commercial loan portfolio. Bancoagricola and BAM also reported NIM compression, mainly reflecting higher funding costs and lower lending and investment income, respectively. On an annual basis, however, while loans are growing at 9.7%, net interest income is increasing almost 16% and underscoring our robust intermediation capacity. Please proceed to Slide 19. Net fee income reached COP 1.4 trillion, increasing 1.6% over the quarter and 15.5% over the year and remains an important and diversified source of revenues. Fee income growth was broad-based.

However, bancassurance continued to stand out, growing 17% over the year and 3% over the quarter supported by the positive performance of our alliance with SURA. Moreover, fee expenses decreased both during the quarter and during the year, driven by ongoing efficiency initiatives. All in all, fee income ratio stood at 21%. Please proceed to Slide 20. Net provision charges reached COP 1.2 trillion, up 17% over the quarter, mainly reflecting an increase in a specific client segment in Bancolombia as well as the consumer segment, driven by higher originations. BAM recorded higher provisions following updates to its expected loss models, incorporating new information for the year while Bancoagricola's provision expenses remained elevated with higher returns adjusted by risk.

Overall, quarterly cost of risk stood at 1.8% and bringing the 9-month accumulated cost of risk to 1.7%. We continue to closely monitor recent events that could affect asset quality. While proactively managing exposures and addressing potentially affected clients. Starting with the earthquake in Colombia, asset quality in the affected areas remains in line with the overall local loan book, supported by relief measures and proactive client management. We may see some deterioration going forward as collections normalize, and we are building provisions accordingly with no expected impact on our cost of risk guidance range for either this year or next year. Regarding El Nino, our main exposure is concentrated in agribusiness, particularly agro exporters.

Provision expenses related to these exposures have already been recognized in line with expected credit losses. Given our limited exposure to potentially affected clients, we do not expect any material additional impact. Please proceed to Slide 21. Delinquency ratios remained well contained across portfolios during the quarter and reflect a meaningful reduction on a yearly basis. Moreover, from an expected losses perspective, Stage 1 has increased significantly its share, driven by growth on originations while Stage 2 and Stage 3 exposures declined over the year. Coverage remained solid at 131% and for 30-day past due loans in 195% for 90-day past due loans.

Overall, these trends indicate that the asset quality remains in line with our guidance considering the pace of growth. Please proceed to Slide 22. Operating expenses remained broadly stable, increasing only 0.3% quarter-over-quarter, driven by higher financial transaction taxes related to the ordinary and extraordinary dividend payments. On a year-to-date basis, expenses grew 8.7%, mainly reflecting the wealth tax recognized earlier this year. Excluding this effect, annual expense growth would have been approximately 5.2%, that is close to 1 percentage point below inflation. Overall, the consolidated cost-to-income ratio stood at 46.6%. We remain focused on structural efficiency and have launched different initiatives addressing our most significant operating and fee related expenses.

In parallel, our AI program is accelerating its adoption across the organization to enhance productivity, supporting a more scalable operating model. Please turn to Slide 23. Shareholders' equity reached COP 39 trillion, up 1.7% over the quarter. Mainly driven by earnings generation, while the year-over-year decline mainly reflects the Banistmo divestment. In July, Bancolombia issued COP 1 billion intra-group capital instrument contributing 44 basis points as additional Tier 1. Combined with the other intragroup transactions, this enhances the group's capital efficiency while providing additional flexibility to support growth in strategic priorities. Bancolombia's stand-alone solvency ratio remained relatively stable at 13.7% as earnings generation and the AT1 issuance helped offset the extraordinary dividend and growth in risk-weighted assets.

Finally, Cibest stand-alone double leverage increased to 98%, reflecting dividend distributions, yet preserving flexibility for corporate initiatives. Please turn to Slide 24. Net income reached COP 2 trillion, which represents an ROE of 22%. The accumulated net income increased 10.5% over the year despite the absence of Banistmo's earnings contribution. And consistently, ROE has jumped from 18% to 22% in the same period underscoring the rationale behind the divestment and the merits of the capital reallocation strategy. With this, I will now hand the presentation back to Juan Carlos. Juan Carlos?

Juan Uribe: Thank you, Mauricio. Please turn to Slide 26. Turning now to our guidance. For 2026, we are revising our loan growth outlook upwards. From a range of 7% to 8% to a range of 8% to 9%. This reflects stronger demand for commercial loans in Colombia. All other guidance items remain unchanged. For 2027, we project a slightly higher loan growth of 9% to 10%, again, led by commercial loans in Colombia, provided the fiscal deficit narrows in an orderly and gradually way. We also expect NIM of 7.5% to 7.7% and a cost of risk of 1.9% to 2.1%, reflecting stronger origination and interest rates that are likely to remain high.

Finally, we expect an efficiency ratio of 46% to 47%. We remain committed to a cost discipline while continuing to invest in our business transformation. Please turn to Slide 27. To conclude, if macroeconomic conditions to improve and investment confidence rebounds strongly, as we anticipate, Grupo Cibest is uniquely well placed to seize the growth opportunities ahead. And even in a more adverse scenario, our proven NIM management, some credit underwriting, cost discipline and efficient capital allocation allow us to deliver sustainable ROE throughout the economic cycle. With this, we conclude our prepared remarks. We will now be pleased to take your questions.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] And our first question will come from Ernesto Gabilondo with Bank of America.

Ernesto María Gabilondo Márquez: Thank you. Hi, good morning, Juan Carlos, Mauricio and Catalina, and thanks for the opportunity to ask questions. My first question will be on your earnings growth expectation for next year. So you have mentioned ROE could be maintained between 21% to 22% next year, but just wondering how should we expect in terms of earnings growth? And for my second question is on Nequi. If I am not mistaken, Nequi represents around 2% of Cibest consolidated net income. So how do you see the Nequi contribution to Cibest's earnings in the future? And wondering if you have any plans to IPO this business in the future? Or if we should just consider it as another subsidiary within Cibest.

Juan Uribe: Thank you, Ernesto. Let me take your question regarding Nequi, and I going to ask Mauricio to take your question about earnings. As you mentioned, Nequi today represents around 2% of Grupo Cibest net income. It's still small. Nequi will grow in the future, and we are very optimistic. But the size of Bancolombia is going to continue to be very important in Cibest. So Nequi will gain some importance in the future, probably in 2030 will be something around 8% to 10%. If the growth is strong, particularly on loans and depending on the credit cycle. But Nequi, it's an alternative.

I mean it's a diversification of Grupo Cibest, but Bancolombia will continue to be the most important entity for Grupo Cibest. But Nequi will add diversification, as I mentioned, and alternatives to have presence in the Colombian market. And also it's going to be an avenue of growth in other markets, particularly in Central America. Regarding the -- your question of our IPO, now Nequi is a separate entity fully owned by Grupo Cibest that give us the opportunity to assess different strategic alternatives. We are not thinking about an IPO in the short term.

But with this corporate structure, we have open alternatives, as I mentioned, to develop Nequi and to find the best way to give Nequi those avenues of growth. So we'll continue developing the business in Colombia and in the other markets that we are. And then in parallel, we will be assessing any additional strategic alternatives, but that is not going to happen in the short term is. Now regarding earnings, Mauricio?

A $35 Million Home Just Hit San Francisco’s Market, Marking the Most Expensive Listing of the Year

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

San Francisco’s most expensive home of the year just hit the market and is expected to draw interest from potential buyers flush with cash from the AI boom.

The home at 2770 Broadway officially went on the market on Monday with a nearly $35 million price tag.

The seven-bedroom, 6.5-bathroom home is already drawing inquiries, listing agent Neil Ward of Compass told SFGate.

Located in the exclusive Gold Coast neighborhood, the home sits on a 50-foot-wide, double-width lot, with breathtaking views of the San Francisco Bay, Golden Gate Bridge, Palace of Fine Arts, and other famed landmarks, according to the listing.

The home was designed in 1894 by Reid & Reid, the same architectural firm responsible for the iconic Fairmont Hotel. While many Gold Coast homes of this age, most of which are generational properties, need complete renovations after being sold, Ward told SFGate that the potential buyer of 2770 Broadway can “walk in with their toothbrush and their furniture.”

A 2007 renovation by architect Jim Jennings and Van Acker Construction brought together the home’s original turn-of-the-century grandeur with the functionality needed for modern family living.

The home’s 9,000 square feet are spread across four floors, where prospective buyers will find herringbone-patterned floors, arched openings, and fine millwork, all evidence of the home’s architectural greatness.

The property also features a two-car garage, wine cellar, and sport court in the rear of the house.

The home was purchased by San Francisco business executive David Chamberlain in 1989, according to SFGate. He was CEO of companies such as Shaklee, Stride Rite, and Genesco, but also had a deep connection with his city, serving as president of the San Francisco Opera and the San Francisco Chamber of Commerce.

After his death earlier this year, Chamberlain’s two daughters decided to sell the home.

Ward told SFGate that he thinks the property will sell for its $35 million asking price, which would make it the most expensive home sold on the multiple listing service in San Francisco this year.

“It is a really, really rare product that does not hit the market,” Ward said.

Between the home’s turnkey condition and the city’s competitive ultraluxury home market, Ward thinks the home’s time on the market will be brief.

“I’ve done this for over 35 years. I was here for the dot-com boom and everything in between. Nothing comes close to what’s taking place right now,” Ward said.

San Francisco is experiencing what some real estate brokerages are calling a “mansion shortage,” according to NPR. The AI boom, much of which is concentrated in San Francisco, has caused demand for luxury single-family homes in the area to skyrocket, while the supply remains stagnant.

Home to major AI companies such as OpenAI and Anthropic, the San Francisco market had a median of just 37 days on the market, according to Realtor.com data, two weeks less than the national median of 61 days.

Some listings have had over 50 offers, and 44 homes were sold for at least $1 million over asking in June alone, according to a report from NPR.

In October, the sale of a home in San Francisco’s Presidio neighborhood broke the record for the highest sale above asking. The 1966 midcentury home was listed for $18.5 million and sold for $28.5 million, an eye-watering $10 million premium.

November Patch Tuesday: An Overflowing Cornucopia of CVEs

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Microsoft serves up an actively exploited Outlook zero-day, and a wave of critical Exchange, SharePoint, Windows Search, and LDAP bugs that exhausted defenders need to prioritize fast.

November offers up a veritable Patch Tuesday feast of vulnerabilities that are more likely to be exploited. In its second four-digit Patch Tuesday release in a row, Microsoft today addressed 1,093 security vulnerabilities, with 63 rated critical, and one zero-day in the mix (CVE-2026-124194, CVSS 6.5) that Microsoft confirmed is under active exploit.

"Most security teams haven’t quite recovered from the last month of bumper-to-bumper critical vulnerabilities, emergency patches, and '2shell'-branded everything, and we're yet again at another Patch Tuesday," says Jake Knott, head of threat intelligence at Watchtowr.

"Teams are really starting to feel the strain of working out what actually matters when there is fire after fire every day demanding immediate attention," he adds. "Our advice hasn't changed … start with what you actually have exposed to the Internet, then what's already being exploited in the wild, and only then everything else. Before going into panic mode, check whether you even use the vulnerable component, or meet the prerequisites for exploitation."

Related:OnlyFans Copyright Takedowns Expose Compromised Websites

To that end, security teams should prioritize CVE-2026-124194, and not just because attackers are already targeting it. It's a spoofing vulnerability in Outlook that may not allow code execution, hence its moderate severity rating, but could allow the disclosure of an account’s NTLMv2 hash — and in turn, the target's login.

"To exploit it, an attacker sends a target a malicious Office file and convinces them to open it," explained Satnam Narang, senior staff research engineer at Tenable, in his monthly Patch Tuesday commentary. "If the attack succeeds, the attacker captures the victim's NT LAN Manager v2 (NTLMv2) hash, a value derived from their Windows password. The attacker may be able to relay it to other systems to sign in as the user, or crack it to recover the password."

Security teams should also prioritize a pair of critical remote code execution (RCE) vulnerabilities, in Exchange and SharePoint, respectively, that each open targets to any attacker with a low-privilege login.

The first, in Exchange Server (CVE-2026-118207, CVSS 8.8), "belongs in this week's emergency window for every Exchange Server Subscription Edition server you run," Automox researchers warned in their monthly Patch Tuesday bulletin, and "all an attacker needs to start is a low-level login."

It's an improper input validation that could allow an authenticated attacker with low-level access to send the server a specially crafted request and run code with no user interaction.

Related:The Evolving Cybersecurity Lexicon: Vulnerability Discovery to Resilience

"Code on an Exchange server reaches every mailbox it hosts," warned Automox researchers. "And Exchange usually holds broad rights in Active Directory, so the mailboxes are just the start."

Meanwhile, an important-rated flaw (CVE-2026-120002, CVSS 9.9) in SharePoint Server is an elevation of privilege vulnerability that needs quick addressing, according to Automox.

"The flaw is incorrect authorization," the researchers noted. "That 9.9 comes from a changed scope: a successful attack reaches past the vulnerable SharePoint component itself. An attacker who already holds a low-privileged account sends requests over the network and elevates their privileges, with no user interaction required."

Another critical RCE to watch out for, according to Automox, is CVE-2026-110043 (CVSS 8.8/10), a one-click heap-based buffer overflow in Outlook that uses the Preview Pane as an attack vector. A target must select a weaponized email so it displays in the Preview Pane for it to trigger the attacker's code, which runs with that user's permissions.

"Patch internet-facing Exchange and SharePoint servers, and book the Exchange restart now," the Automox researchers advised. "Push the Office update this week, then check the installed build, because a fully patched Windows dashboard says nothing about Office. Rank by score and assessment, not label."

Related:Ivanti ICS Zero-Day Attacks Highlight Vendor Patching Hurdles

Dustin Childs, head of threat awareness at the Zero-Day Initiative (ZDI), also identified a Windows Search RCE (CVE-2026-116928, CVSS 9.8) as needing immediate attention.

"We start with Windows Search, which is reached through Exchange Server," he said in his November Patch Tuesday blog post, where he also listed 30 potentially wormable CVEs to patch quickly. "This bug allows an unauthenticated attacker to send an email with a specially crafted attachment that triggers the bug when the server automatically indexes it. The recipient never opens anything. It is a zero-click code execution bug on a high-value target. It’s also a CVSS 9.8 and clearly more severe than any of the nine patches Exchange receives this month."

And finally, Childs pointed to CVE-2026-119958, an RCE bug in Windows Lightweight Directory Access Protocol (LDAP).

"This patch addresses a bug that allows an unauthenticated attacker to exploit an out-of-bounds (OOB) write (CWE-787) by sending crafted LDAP calls over the network, with no user interaction," he warned. "Since it runs code in the LDAP service context, it could be quite destructive, especially on Domain Controllers. Test and deploy this one quickly."

Childs also recommended testing and deploying patches for the wormable bugs quickly, especially for network-facing services like DNS, DHCP, LDAP, and Exchange.

White the cornucopia of CVE this month, especially on the heels of the record October Patch Tuesday, may feel overwhelming, security teams should stick to best practices and not get overwhelmed, advises Tyler Reguly, associate director of security R&D at Fortra.

"I don't know about everyone else, but I'm tired," he said via email, adding that he believes that, eventually, AI will finish turning up old bugs and teams can once again look forward to smaller Patch Tuesday releases. "I can only imagine that if I’m tired, admins and operations teams everywhere are also tired. I believe that we will make it through to the other side, if we prioritize updates and focus on what really matters. The noise is coming at us from all sides, and we need to cut through it and figure out what really matters to the business."

Tara Seals is an award-winning journalist with 25+ years of experience as a reporter, analyst, and editor in the cybersecurity, communications, and technology spaces. As managing editor, she runs the newsroom at Dark Reading, leading a team of staff writers and freelance contributors. 📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., told Fox News she is not concerned that the federal investigations into her immigration status and financial filings will distract her from serving her constituents in Congress following a lengthy meeting with her lawyers.

Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., returned to Capitol Hill this week and brought two lawyers with her after a scandal-plagued fall recess.

Fox News spotted the two attorneys exiting her Washington, D.C., office Tuesday after what appeared to be a roughly two-hour meeting with the congresswoman.

Omar hired two high-profile attorneys, Amy Jeffress and Joshua Matz, both of whom are partners at Heckler Fink LLP, amid dueling investigations. After emerging from Omar’s office on Tuesday, Fox News asked about their confidence in Omar’s defense against the federal government.

OBAMA ADMIN FIRST LAUNCHED THE INVESTIGATION INTO ILHAN OMAR'S ALLEGED BROTHER MARRIAGE

The attorneys were also asked about the recent news that both the Obama and Biden administrations had been the first to investigate Omar after she claimed that the Trump administration was politically targeting her.

Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., speaks during a news conference Wednesday in which she blamed President Donald Trump for death threats and attacks against her. (AP Photo/Adam Gray)

This is the first sighting of Omar back on the Hill after two federal departments opened investigations into the Somali-born congresswoman looking into both her immigration status and previous financial filings.

In October, agents with Homeland Security Investigations (HSI) raided her Washington, D.C., home as part of what Fox News Digital learned was an investigation into whether she or her father committed fraud when filling out her immigration paperwork.

She spent the final weeks of her campaign asking for money to defend herself in the investigations and accusing Trump of targeting her for political reasons.

About an hour after Omar's lawyers exited her office Tuesday, the congresswoman departed with husband Tim Mynett.

She was approached by Fox News about the ongoing investigation and asked whether she is concerned it could affect her ability to effectively represent her constituents in Congress.

She was then asked if there was any chance she would step down from Congress despite just winning re-election to let the investigation "play out."

The congresswoman smiled and waved at the camera but did not respond as the elevator doors closed with her inside.

SCANDAL-PLAGUED ILHAN OMAR EASILY SECURES 5TH TERM IN DEEP BLUE MINNESOTA DESPITE IMMIGRATION FRAUD PROBE

Rep. Ilhan Omar, D-Minn., pictured walking and talking in the U.S. Capitol basement. (Fox News Digital)

Omar also refused to respond to Fox’s question about whether she had spoken to House Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries about the investigation.

The attorneys seen exiting her office earlier, Jeffress and Matz, have both represented high-ranking Democratic elected officials and have taken on multiple cases against President Donald Trump, his allies and supporters.

Jeffress served as former President Joe Biden’s attorney in his lawsuit against the Justice Department earlier this year. She also served as counsel for then-House Speaker Nancy Pelosi and the House committee investigating the Jan. 6, 2021, riot on Capitol Hill.

She is also married to U.S. District Judge Christopher Cooper of the District of Washington, who ruled in May that Trump's addition his name to the John F. Kennedy Performing Arts Center was illegal.

Howls, Growls, and Sounds of Hounds

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Should you use an external loudspeaker while on trail? The internet has spoken: you should not. Should you use headphones? Conclusions vary, but in general, no. Sometimes the internet provides a good reason, and sometimes just an opinion.

Thru-hiking has its mundane moments. And I don’t just mean road-walks – several years ago, I was bored at least once on the John Muir Trail. A few thousand miles later, even though boredom is still very much a thing, I’m able to zone-out more easily. Unless, that is, I get an earworm. Then, my daydreaming gets interrupted.

It was a cold morning on the PCT. I broke camp, started hiking, and realized I’d left my MP3 player in the side pocket of my tent. Digging it out was too much hassle, so I hiked in silence. I noticed a patch of frost within a patch of shade, and that’s how the earworm gained entry. The song that got stuck in my head was “Frosty the S**wman”, and I’m hesitant to name it because it’s so virulent. The first couple of lines rattled around in my skull for the next ten miles. That was the day I learned that listening to music is a good defense against earworms.

The day after the bear incident, I mentally survived a hot, 15 mile road-walk, and physically survived the high speed dump-trucks on County Highway F. Unsettled weather arrived that night, and when I hit the trail the next day, distant thunder started approaching from the west after 30 minutes. The air remained warm even as the storm eventually passed overhead, but the torrential rain was extremely cold. The trail turned into a stream in some places, and a pond in others. Wet feet, no problem. When my music cut out, that was a problem.

My jacket pocket wasn’t as waterproof as I thought, and I discovered that my MP3 player was completely waterlogged. Despite the water sloshing around inside, I was hopeful it might eventually dry out. I shook out as much water as possible before putting it away.

The MP3 player spent that night inside one of my sleep-socks, on top of my foot, like a penguin egg. I incubated it in my shorts pocket the next day, while hiking the Mondeaux River eskers. There was a minor earworm infestation, but I’ve had worse.

At the beginning of the third day, I made a beeline for the town of Rib Lake. It was a full day of distractions.

At the end of the third day, resurrection! Kind of. The MP3 player was able to play music, even though one of its buttons didn’t work. And its display soon flickered and died. It was stuck in “Shuffle” mode, and had developed a couple of other quirks, but it worked well enough. It defended me valiantly against earworms for the remainder of my thru-hike.

I headed north out of town along Kennedy Street. During the 1.5 mile road-walk, three pickup trucks drove past. Each truck was outfitted with several kennels, and each kennel contained an excited, baying hound. As the noise faded into the distance, I reached the trailhead, headed east, and silence descended. With some background music, of course.

That afternoon, I met hikers Linda and Wendy, who were heading west. They were piecing together the entire IAT by parking a vehicle at each of two trailheads, and hiking from one to the next. I met them again at Wood Lake County Park campground, and they were very hospitable. They let me share their campsite, and that evening, Wendy cooked Gnocchi, Pesto, and Chicken of the Woods (mushrooms that she’d picked that day).

In the morning, after only two miles, I saw the three pickup trucks again, empty this time. They were parked at my first trailhead of the day, and I could hear the hounds’ sounds coming from the direction I needed to go. Whether they were training or hunting, I wasn’t sure. Randomly, my MP3 player chose that moment to select “Dueling Banjos”.

Not really. But it would have been appropriate.

I decided to walk the road for a few miles, heading south. Again, the noise faded into the distance.

Compared to western states, Wisconsin sounds different. This was day 17, and I was still noticing birdsongs I hadn’t heard before. The R2-D2 bird, for example. I’d recently discovered that the creatures snorting at me from afar were White-tailed bucks, who apparently have an excellent sense of smell. Mule deer out west have never snorted at me.

In camp, at sunset, three wolf-packs exchanged howls several miles away. It was the only time on the Ice Age Trail that I heard wolves, so they’re probably no more common in the midwest than the west.

The next day, I hiked north along the west bank of the Wisconsin River, then south along the other side. The Grandfather Falls dam and hydropower plant were interesting, but the subsequent out-and-back was not, at least according to FarOut commenters. I know the IAT is a work-in-progress, but I believe a three mile round-trip with no pay-off is optional for thru-hikers. I skipped it, and immediately started a 10 mile road-walk.

I dodged most of a heavy rain shower by sheltering in the pavilion at Tug Lake County Park, and was dry by the time I reached my campsite, which was, in this case, a shelter. It was my first ever night in one – cozy, even if the neighbors were noisy. I now know that the birds at Dog Lake that night were Sandhill cranes.

The next night, my neighbors were remarkably quiet. At the campsite on the other side of Chain Lake, about 300 yards away, were 16 teenagers and two camp counselors from Milwaukee. It sounded like everyone was fast asleep as soon as the sun went down.

I only hitched once on the IAT, going to Rhinelander. I stayed the night, and tried hitching back mid-morning. There were roadworks on the edge of town, which might be why state route 17 wasn’t very busy. An hour later, I had no takers. I tried Uber, also unsuccessfully, then called a cab. Five minutes later, they picked me up. I was back on trail at midday, and reached a suitable place to camp about five o’clock.

Actually, suitable is an exaggeration. I chose a flat patch of grass beside a five-way intersection of overgrown, disused dirt roads. I wouldn’t have camped there if this had been Grizzly territory.

As expected, the intersection was busy that night, but each disturbance didn’t keep me awake for long. Then, at 1:15 AM, something started growling at me, and I was wide awake in an instant, heart thumping.

I’ve been growled at by dogs and coyotes, and grumbled at by porcupines and raccoons. This sound wasn’t canine, and the animal making it didn’t sound small. In my imagination, the creature was bear-sized.

I used my outside voice, but there was no reaction. I unzipped my tent door and shone my flashlight around. Still no movement. I couldn’t see the animal because it was on the other side of my tent. Periodically, for about 15 minutes, it uttered a low, rumbling growl lasting several seconds. Each time, I replied with a few words.

Then, at last, it padded quietly away. I lay awake for 45 minutes while the adrenaline subsided. In the morning, when I had cell service, I researched my nocturnal visitor, and suddenly everything made sense. Wisconsin is the Badger State.

Fast-forward a few hours, and I had a silent night. Wholly unexciting night. All was calm, all was quite… normal. The morning was a bit chilly, but that’s hardly worth mentioning.

In the afternoon, near the tiny community of Polar, I arrived at my chosen campsite on the shore of Mueller Lake. On the water, I could hear a couple of speedboats, their stereos, and the shrieks of people enjoying themselves. The boaters soon went home, and for a time, it was peaceful.

Then the residents of the lakefront house 400 feet away fired up their megawatt outdoor sound system. For the next three hours, I was treated to a Spotify 70’s selection with commercial breaks. Full-size headphones with noise cancelling would have been useful. Fortunately, the loudspeakers fell silent just before sunset.

So, if someone were to suggest that earbuds disconnect me from the sounds of nature, I’d have to disagree. They don’t disconnect me from man-made sounds either.

Sometimes you’ll hear logging on the IAT.

The buyer pays the same price as they would otherwise, and your purchase helps to support The Trek's ongoing goal to serve you quality backpacking advice and information. Thanks for your support!

Useless fact: I named my left quad "brute-force" and my right quad "ignorance". Alternative fact: originally from the UK, I've lived in the USA for over twenty years. I've spent most of my trail-time in the western states of the US, but also hiked in Scotland, Canada and New Zealand. I planned on being in the PCT class of 2020, but had to postpone. My 2021 SOBO attempt was interrupted by fires and closures. In comparison, the AZT, CDT, PNT, and IAT were almost problem-free. --Richard, aka "LowRange"

NASA Pushes Further Ahead With The Non-Space Side Of Its Mission, Teases New Experimental Plane

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

NASA has unveiled, sort of, its newest experimental plane. And by that, the agency appears to mean a plane meant to conduct experiments, rather than the plane itself being the experiment. Called the X-70 Fractal, the aircraft's primary selling point is its modular design, both inside and out. This means that payloads can be swapped out easily, whether they are hanging off the wings or nestled inside the plane. Even the flight controls can be reconfigured in case the agency wants to test different ways for the pilots (and it is piloted) to maneuver. The X-70 is really a platform to allow for a wide array of experiments on a single airframe, rather than creating a bespoke airframe to conduct one mission, like the X-59 is doing now.

As for the plane itself, the X-70 Fractal is... well, more announcement than vehicle, at least for now. NASA says that it can carry 2,000 pounds of science equipment and take off with up to 10,000 pounds total. It will fly up to 45,000 feet in altitude, far above most airliners and well into the stratosphere. It will go up to 1,500 nautical miles, broadly on par with an F-35. It also looks like an F-35, I think, maybe? For images, all we've got is this misty photo that looks AI generated. NASA seems to want to copy the energy of the F-47 and F/A-XX reveals, which are also foggy renderings and not actual photographs. Show me you're making an airplane without showing me you're making an airplane, I guess.

NASA achieves such historic feats in space that it's easy to forget that it is also an "Aeronautics" administration. Part of its remit is to push forward the boundaries of aviation here on this planet, which NASA Administrator Jared Isaacman has renewed focus on. Isaacman himself is a pilot who owns his own MiG-29 Soviet fighter jet, so this is an area of personal interest for him. In fact, he's promising that more experimental aircraft are on their way; rumors abound that the agency may be returning the famous SR-71 Blackbird, the fastest plane ever made, to service.

By doing so, NASA does important R&D on airplane technology that is then released into the public record, to the benefit of all aviation companies and everyone who flies on their products. That's the whole point of the X-59, which is trying to reduce the impact of sonic booms enough to make commercial supersonic planes financially viable. In addition, NASA's latest budget points to aviation as an area of strategic competition, per Wired. To that end, it may do some research alongside the Pentagon as well. This is where something like the X-70 is useful: it could do scientific, commercial, or military research as needed, simply by swapping out equipment payloads via its modular design. Now all NASA needs to do is, you know, build it, possibly with a contractor. Details!

The Met Opera is using YouTube to look for a new generation of fans

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Timothee Chalamet may think that no one cares about opera, but one of the most prominent opera houses in the U.S. is betting that it can find new fans for a 430-year-old art form — it just needs to look in the right place. New York’s Metropolitan Opera is delivering a YouTube live stream that will provide open access to a performance of the new work Lincoln in the Bardo.

The Lincoln in the Bardo opera, which is based on a bestselling novel by George Saunders, had its world premiere at the Met Opera last month. Now, as the 143-year-old opera company looks to expand its fan base, it is hosting a Lincoln in the Bardo live stream on its official YouTube channel. The broadcast is set to go live on November 14 and will remain available until the 18th. Beginning on November 20, the Lincoln in the Bardo stream will transition to paid formats.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

The YouTube stream isn’t just a play for new fans — it’s also a fundraiser for the host company. The Met Opera is tying its free broadcast to its Make the Met Forever fundraising campaign, which aims to bring in resources that can support Met programming through 2030. As part of the stream, viewers will be encouraged to make donations via chat.

“With the final performances of our run completely sold out, we decided to share Lincoln in the Bardo for free with our global audience,” set Meta Opera General Manager Peter Gelb in a statement provided to OperaWire. “Opera is being transformed by powerful new works like Lincoln in the Bardo, and we hope our efforts will be rewarded with donations to our Make the Met Forever fundraising campaign.”

If the Met is looking to present dramatic fare that can get younger generations excited about the medium, then Lincoln in the Bardo is an excellent choice. Saunders’ novel won the Booker Prize, and Dua Lipa‘s Service95 venture chronicled the operatic adaptation through a mini documentary. The Met Opera will stream the doc during the YouTube broadcast’s intermission.

There are several barriers that make it difficult to market opera to today’s young people, and the medium’s elitist airs present one of those roadblocks. By making Lincoln in the Bardo available to all, the Met is showing that its performances can have broad appeal — and that promoting opera doesn’t have to be as difficult as getting your son back from beyond the grave.

MARA dumps Bitcoin to erase a $750M collateral risk

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

MARA repaid its Bitcoin-backed loans, but 2027 bond puts, cash resources and spending choices shape the case for further sales.

Bitcoin miner MARA Holdings has repaid its $750 million of Bitcoin-backed loans, ending the collateral-liquidation exposure attached to those facilities.

Further coin sales could now serve to build cash for bondholder demands, AI infrastructure, and possible share buybacks.

The company's Nov. 9 filing disclosed that it sold 13,597 Bitcoin after Sept. 30 for approximately $1.1 billion and used $750 million to repay the loans in full.

Management's quarterly letter says it intends to use Bitcoin to build cash for AI infrastructure opportunities, convertible-note puts exercisable next year and potential repurchases of its own shares.

As of Sept. 30, MARA had $291.6 million of June 2031 notes carrying a June 4, 2027 cash-put right, and $632.5 million of March 2030 notes carrying a December 1, 2027 right. Together, those September balances total about $924.1 million.

A cash put lets a bondholder require the company to buy back all or part of the notes for cash at face value, plus any applicable unpaid special interest.

The notes' contractual maturity dates remain in 2030 and 2031, and the eventual cash bill depends on which holders exercise and how much principal remains outstanding.

A separate obligation of $48 million of notes outstanding at Sept. 30 matures on Dec. 1. The put calendar does not require MARA to sell Bitcoin to meet potential cash demands.

The 2027 holder-put calendar survives the repayment of MARA's Bitcoin-backed loans. Explore how the potential route to selling changed.

The outstanding Coinbase and Two Prime loans exposed pledged Bitcoin to conditional lender liquidation remedies.

The Nov. 9 disclosures report full repayment after quarter-end. Management still identifies cash uses for continued monetization.

Cash puts are elective holder rights. Repayment removes one liquidation channel while management still plans Bitcoin monetization.

Principal only; applicable accrued unpaid special interest is additional. Payments depend on holder exercise and balances at the dates. Loan payoff occurred after Sept. 30, disclosed Nov. 9; exact transaction dates were not supplied. No current cash balance or inevitable future Bitcoin sale is implied.

MARA entered the fourth quarter with $946.5 million of cash and equivalents, excluding $12 million of restricted cash. Approximately 11% of that cash was held in euros by its majority-owned subsidiary Exaion and contractually designated for its operations over the next year.

The subsequent Bitcoin sales brought in approximately $350 million more than the loan payoff. Adding that difference to September cash produces a rough $1.3 billion bridge before other cash movements.

MARA also reported about $1.5 billion of unused capacity under its at-the-market share issuance program as of Sept. 30. Management said existing liquidity resources provided flexibility to meet obligations and fund growth over the next twelve months and beyond.

MARA used $642.7 million of cash in operating activities during the first nine months of 2026. Its Bitcoin-sale proceeds are classified as investing cash flows, so the operating outflow alone cannot establish a funding gap after those sales.

The planned acquisition of power business Long Ridge remained pending in the Nov. 9 disclosures. Its approximately $1.5 billion enterprise value includes up to about $900 million of assumed debt.

A bridge commitment of up to $785 million supports a portion of that debt if needed, subject to customary conditions.

The data-center development agreement with Starwood includes MARA funds approved for pre-development pursuit costs, with $136.3 million approved across sites and about $17.7 million incurred by Sept. 30.

Site contributions receive value before MARA adds incremental equity, while project financing is expected to be non-recourse.

The HIF site-development project's price of up to $600 million is tied to post-closing development milestones, with additional payments possible for specified shortfalls.

The board's Nov. 9 authorization permits up to $500 million of common-share purchases, but requires no particular amount and can be suspended or terminated.

The next meaningful supply signal is MARA's disclosure of further Bitcoin sales and how it uses the proceeds. Updated cash and remaining note balances will help distinguish preparation for holder puts from optional infrastructure and buyback spending.

SpaceX moves Starship Flight 15 booster to launch pad for 'squeeze test' ahead of epic double-catch attempt (photos)

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe

Start exploring exclusive deals, expert advice and more

Space news, cosmic updates and expert insights straight to your inbox.

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The historic flight is scheduled to launch on Friday (Nov. 13).

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

SpaceX is one big step closer to the 15th test flight of its Starship megarocket.

The company announced today (Nov. 10) that it had moved its latest Starship first stage, known as Super Heavy Booster 22, to the launch mount at its Starbase facility in South Texas ahead of Flight 15, which is scheduled to lift off this Friday (Nov. 13).

During the upcoming launch test, SpaceX plans to catch both Booster 22 and Starship's Ship upper stage with its launch tower's "chopstick" arms in a double landing catch — something the private spaceflight company has never tried before. It will be the first full recovery of an orbital rocket in history.

Booster 22 therefore "was used for final catch testing upon arrival [at the pad], including a suspended squeeze test at catch height," SpaceX said in an X post today (Nov. 10).

That post shared four photos of the giant booster, which is powered by 33 of SpaceX's next-gen Raptor engines. The first shot showed Super Heavy in one of Starbase's "Mega Bay" assembly buildings before rollout, while the other three depicted it grasped by the tower's arms, held high in the South Texas sky.

SpaceX has used the chopstick arms during Starship flights before; it has successfully caught Super Heavy on three separate occasions. But the company has never attempted a catch of Ship, which flies higher and farther than the booster and is therefore a more challenging target. And SpaceX only reached orbit with Starship for the first time on Flight 14, which launched on Sept. 28.

So Flight 15 will break new ground. And, if all goes to plan, it will give us a look at the future: SpaceX plans to catch Super Heavy and Ship on all operational Starship missions, as this recovery strategy will allow the company to boost the megarocket's launch cadence.

And SpaceX wants to fly Starship incredibly often, so that it can build out its Starlink V3 broadband constellation and even bigger Starmind AI data-center network. Indeed, Elon Musk has said the company is eyeing 10,000 Starship flights per year by 2030.

If all goes to plan, Flight 15 will launch from Starbase on Friday during a 75-minute window that opens at 5:45 p.m. EST (2245 GMT; 4:45 p.m. local Texas time).

Super Heavy will return to Starbase for its catch about 6.5 minutes after liftoff. Ship will follow suit much later — about 22 hours later, in fact, after it has deployed 26 Starlink V3 spacecraft into LEO and completed 14 circuits of our planet.

You can watch all the action live here at Space.com and get updates about the mission here.

These Underrated Tools Are Up to 39% Off Ahead of Black Friday

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Shop Black Friday savings on clever, overlooked tools that make everyday projects easier–and prove that the little guys deserve some glory, too.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. Why Trust Us?

Every toolbox has its stars. The cordless drill, the circular saw, the impact driver. Big, powerful tools that get most of the attention. But tucked away in the corners of my own tool drawers are the unsung heroes—those little problem solvers that I barely think of until that moment I desperately need them. And when I do—Wow—I sure am glad they’re there.

I’m talking about the clever tools specifically designed for certain situations that make everyday jobs easier. A Klein Voltage Tester so I don’t mistakenly zap myself as I replace a wall receptacle. A Bosch Laser Measure that saves me from wrestling a tape across a ceiling. Or a Japanese-style pull saw that makes clean, precise cuts the old-school way—by hand. These might not be the flashiest tools in my shop, but I’ve come to dedicate a singular drawer to them as I use them so often.

With Black Friday deals already rolling in, now’s a great time to give these underrated workhorses a little love. I’ve rounded up a few of my favorite useful, affordable unsung-hero-type tools that are currently enjoying some pretty significant discounts. They’re great gifts for the DIYer who seems to have everything, and even better excuses to treat yourself to a tool that perhaps you didn’t know you needed.

Here’s a holiday tool purchase that won’t hurt your gift budget. For less than $20 on sale, this little Klein Voltage Tester deserves a spot in your handyperson’s toolbox. Touch the receiver to a live wire, outlet, or switch and the tester lights up and beeps, indicating a flow of current. I keep mine right in its own small pocket in my tool belt, and another in my tool drawer so it’s an easy grab to make sure that wire isn’t lighting me up in addition to the fixture it’s connected to. Is it a “head-turner” gift? Not really. Is it a gift that will be appreciated for years to come? You bet.

I’m a huge fan of these Craftsman Ratcheting Wrenches, and at 36 percent off, my enthusiasm is growing. There’s nothing like trying to loosen a stubborn bolt in a cramped space, where you barely have room to fit a conventional ratchet, let alone your knuckles. That’s where these wrenches shine. Each has a 90-tooth mechanism requiring only four degrees of swing to engage the next tooth, which makes work in tight quarters possible. This 20-piece set includes both metric and SAE, so you should be covered in terms of sizes across the board. At this price, it’s a lot of wrench for the money.

Ever stretched a tape measure across a wall or ceiling only to have it buckle halfway there? So much for your measurement–and your patience. At just around $50, this Bosch is a smart holiday tool for anyone measuring rooms for flooring, paint, or furniture. It measures distances up to 100 feet and comes with two AA batteries so your lucky DIY enthusiast can put it straight to work.

Nothing ruins your day quite like that little Check Engine light coming on. But before you pay your mechanic just to tell you what’s wrong, plug in this code reader. The ANCEL AD310 reads engine fault codes, gives you a bit of insight into what’s happening beneath the hood, and can clear codes as well. No batteries. No subscriptions. No Bluetooth pairing required. Just more information about what’s going on with your car or truck, which—at $30—is a pretty sweet little tool for a home mechanic, or anyone who wants to know where they stand before taking their vehicle to a shop.

If you’re building a birdhouse, repairing furniture, or taking your first shot at joinery, here’s an inexpensive way to get acquainted with a pull saw if you’ve never owned one before. This $18 saw from RUITOOL is a great entry point for beginning woodworkers–and useful as well for DIYers making small, precision cuts. Its thin blade cuts on the pull-stroke, with separate edges for more difficult cuts across the grain. At more than 20 percent off, it’s an affordable addition to anyone’s woodworking toolbox.

When I first started working at Popular Mechanics, this Klein Hybrid Screwdriver was up for grabs. I practically looked over both shoulders before claiming it. Years of working with tools have taught me to spot a good one, and this one had me at “Free”. It has onboard bit storage, nut drivers, and those bits are impact-rated to boot. Maybe it’s not exactly free, but at $20 for Black Friday, you, too, can have your own little “wait, seriously??” moment.

This is probably the most basic pocket hole jig that Kreg makes. I use mine all the time, mostly because it’s simple to work with. Maybe you’re building a cabinet and you want to hide a fastener in a pre-drilled pocket hole–or have another situation that calls for a hidden screw that won’t crack the material. Simply set the jig stop against the edge of your workpiece, clamp it in place, and use the enclosed drill bit to bore through the guide and into your wood. Perfect pocket holes every time, ready to accept–and hide–your fastener of choice.

These extra-slim pliers are great for reaching nuts and fittings in tight spots. Think plumbing repairs, awkward connections, and spaces where a conventional wrench just won’t cut it. The rubber grips are comfortable on the hands, and I like the serrated teeth in the jaws for gripping and pulling nails (there’s even a handy groove for holding nails and screws in place as you start to drive them). They’re not particularly flashy, but they are a genuinely useful problem-solver. And at nearly 40 percent off, they certainly deserve that spot in my tool drawer.

Have you ever cut a baseboard miter on an outside corner, only to discover when you go to nail it that there’s a sizable gap in the joint? Odds are your miter saw isn’t wrong–the construction of the wall corner is. Sure, there are methods for stacking and scribing lines on the trim to determine the exact angle–or you could simply reach for this Digital Angle Finder by Klein and uncover the truth in seconds. Use this once and you’ll come to realize that caulk isn’t your best friend in these situations–this angle finder is.

Meta warns some of its AI glasses may get 'too warm' if they get wet

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The company is offering free replacements to affected devices.

Meta is rolling out a software update for its AI glasses to fix an issue that causes some devices to become "too warm" when they get wet. The company describes it as a "rare issue" affecting only a "small number" of its glasses, but is nonetheless urging everyone with a pair of Meta Glasses to update their software and offering free replacements to those affected.

The company has begun notifying users about the forthcoming update which will roll out to everyone with a pair of Meta-branded frames, which includes Adventurer, Fury, Starfire, Nova and Capri models. Oakley and Ray-Ban glasses are not impacted, according to Meta.

While Meta doesn't use the word "overheating," the company compares its glasses fix to how smartphones automatically shut down when they get too hot. "We've implemented a software update to fix a rare issue that caused a small number of Meta Glasses to get too warm when moisture entered the device," a Meta spokesperson said in a statement. "It works similarly to a phone shutting off automatically when it needs to cool down. If people experience this, they can contact Meta Customer Service for a replacement."

In an email to users reported by 9to5Google, Meta says that the heat-related issue can be triggered by "excessive moisture or sweat" entering the glasses. That suggests that at least some of the company's own AI glasses may not be as water resistant as they're supposed to be. According to Meta, all of its AI glasses come with IPX4 water resistance which "means your glasses are safe if you get caught in light rain while you wear them."

The company hasn't indicated how many pairs of glasses might be affected, but says that those who experience the moisture-related shutdowns will be able to get a free replacement pair of glasses. Affected glasses will show a push notification if they do turn off unexpectedly, and the alert will direct them to Meta's customer support.

In the meantime, the company is urging everyone with a pair of Meta Glasses to update to the latest software, version 130, to ensure "the best performance." You can update your glasses from the Meta AI app by heading to Settings > Glasses > Updates. The version 130 update is still rolling out so it may take a day or so to hit everyone.

Have you experienced a pair of Meta Glasses getting too hot or unexpectedly shutting down? Contact the author at karissa [at] engadget.com or karissabe.50 on Signal.
